> COMMENT: Sadly, Peter Clarke passed away on 31 July 2025 at age of 66, after a
>          long illness. Peter Clarke's own comments are denoted (PC).
>
> COMMENT: "It's a common misconception that Mark Cooksey did the music -
>          actually Mark did the sound effects, whilst Peter Clarke composed the
>          music. The reason I know this is because I wrote the music player for
>          it. The story goes that myself and Pete where hired to do the music
>          for £600. When we delivered they turned around and said, ahh yes but
>          we've had to pay £200 to get the sound effects done so here's
>          £400)..." (Paul Hughes)

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/C/Cooksey_Mark/
> COMMENT: Mark Cooksey's own comments are denoted (MC).
>
> COMMENT: "This game was based on the well known board game. Tune 2 is a weird
>          victory ditty that was composed by my brother. Tune 3 I think is the
>          highscore music and tune 4 is the ingame music, just a 2 channel
>          ambient track which left 1 channel for sfx. Tune 5 seems to be corrupt
>          and out of sync for some reason but was meant to be used for action
>          parts of the game." (MC)
> (#5)
> COMMENT: On the original tape and disk, 6 bytes are corrupt, causing the
>          arpeggio to fall out of sync at 0:05 like Mark says. This rip replaces
>          them by 6 bytes that are repeated before and after.

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/C/Cooksey_Mark/
> COMMENT: Mark Cooksey's own comments are denoted (MC).
>
> COMMENT: "I remember this project very well because most of Elite went down to
>          the 20th Cent Fox HQ in London. We had a private pre-release screening
>          of Gremlins II in their mini cinema, kitted out with surround sound.
>          The computer press were invited and Steve Wilcox gave a talk before
>          the film was screened. After wards we went to a Thai restaurant but
>          being a potatoes &amp; gravy man I passed on the food and hot footed it
>          home. Worryingly, listening to the music I'm not 100% sure if I did it
>          or not. I think I did cause certain elements sound like my music but
>          the highscore tune (No 6) sounds far too good. Perhaps by 1990 I had
>          improved the music player routine and decided to do a good job for
>          this game." (MC)
> (#1)
>   TITLE: Gremlins Credits [from the movie Gremlins 2]
>  ARTIST: Jerry Goldsmith

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/G/Galway_Martin/
> COMMENT: Martin Galway's own comments are denoted (MG).
>          "I will never do rearrangements of those tunes on other instruments,
>          the SID is what they're meant to be heard on. It would not be
>          'updating' them to put them onto a different instrument. If you ever
>          hear music from me on today's instruments, the tunes will be all-new
>          (and probably a lot better)." (MG)
>
> COMMENT: "Can't quite remember what happened with 'Ping Pong'. I seem to
>          remember not having very much time to work on it. I do recall working
>          late on it after everyone else had left, because perhaps I was going
>          on holiday the next day or something. (Was it around September-1987?)
>          The arcade game didn't have any music on the 'press start' screen, so
>          it would have been a natural for an original tune. Maybe everyone
>          regarded it as a 'baby game' which didn't merit much attention. CAN'T
>          QUITE REMEMBER, sorry!" (MG)

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/G/Galway_Martin/
> COMMENT: Martin Galway's own comments are denoted (MG).
>          "I will never do rearrangements of those tunes on other instruments,
>          the SID is what they're meant to be heard on. It would not be
>          'updating' them to put them onto a different instrument. If you ever
>          hear music from me on today's instruments, the tunes will be all-new
>          (and probably a lot better)." (MG)
>
> COMMENT: "Once I had finished on 'Roland's Rat Race', which took a month,
>          [Ocean] agreed to suspend my tasks as a programmer and my employment
>          as game sound guy began." (MG)
>          "[...] the sounds are a bit poor. I didn't develop pulse-width
>          modulation until the next project, which at the time was called
>          'Cyclone'. It later came out as 'Helicopter Jagd'. On 'Roland's Rat
>          Race' I guess you could say I was still mastering the C64. It was
>          January/February 1985, my first project on staff at Ocean (I had done
>          quite a few before that as a freelancer but they weren't comfortable
>          employing someone SOLELY TO DO MUSIC... those were the days!)." (MG)
> (#1)
>   TITLE: No.1 Ratfan [from The Cassette of the Album]
>  ARTIST: P. Hammond and R. Matthews, performed by Roland Rat Superstar (David Claridge)
> COMMENT: The first 23 seconds are taken from the 12" version of "No.1 Ratfan".
> (#3)
> COMMENT: Same as /MUSICIANS/G/Galway_Martin/Hyper_Sports.sid, Tune #25.
> (#4)
>   TITLE: Bonus Stage [from the arcade game Super Locomotive]

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/H/Hubbard_Rob/
> COMMENT: People have often stolen Hubbard's routine causing some tunes to be
>          falsely credited to him.
>          Hubbard's own comments are denoted (RH).
>
> COMMENT: "There is an interesting story behind Commando. I went down to their
>          office and started working on it late at night, and worked on it
>          through the night. I took one listen to the original arcade version
>          and started working on the C64 version. I think they wanted some
>          resemblance to the arcade version, but I just did what I wanted to do.
>          By the time everyone arrived at 8.00am in the morning, I had loaded
>          the main tune on every C64 in the building! I got my cheque and was on
>          a train home by 10.00 am..." (RH)

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/H/Hubbard_Rob/
> COMMENT: People have often stolen Hubbard's routine causing some tunes to be
>          falsely credited to him.
>          Hubbard's own comments are denoted (RH).
>
> COMMENT: "Last V8 was a simple futuristic game and so I was off into Jarre
>          land.... I purposely wrote the 2 melody lines to work off one voice to
>          make it sound like more than 1 voice." (RH)
>          The Last V8 was the first game released by Mastertronic's new label
>          MAD (Mastertronic's Added Dimension). Due to the fact they wanted the
>          launch to coincide with the release of the Last V8, the game was rush
>          released and therefore unfortunately had a bug, where the
>          main theme music (subtune #1) falls out of synch at around the one
>          minute mark. Upon closer investigation, this is due to a mere seven
>          bytes of the music player code being incorrect. This SID has those
>          bytes corrected.

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/H/Hubbard_Rob/
> COMMENT: People have often stolen Hubbard's routine causing some tunes to be
>          falsely credited to him.
>          Hubbard's own comments are denoted (RH).
>
> COMMENT: "Last V8 was a simple futuristic game and so I was off into Jarre
>          land.... I purposely wrote the 2 melody lines to work off one voice to
>          make it sound like more than 1 voice." (RH)
>          The Commodore 128 disk-only version had different speech samples from
>          the Commodore 64 version. However it still also exhibited the same
>          bug with tune #1 (see /MUSICIANS/H/Hubbard_Rob/Last_V8.sid) which
>          has been corrected here too.

>          "OMAHD was [...] Jarre inspired. It was actually based on something I
>          wrote a few years before, so it was from music I had already written
>          (as was Chimera and Phantoms of the Asteroids)." (RH)

>          just fitted the tempo and feel so well...." (RH)

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/L/Leitch_Barry/
> COMMENT: All Barry's comments are indicated by (BL).
>
> COMMENT: "I got the one of the highest ratings I've ever got for this.. Zzap
>          64 gave me 96% for it.. one of the others was Impossamole on the NEC.
>          I have a copy of that somewhere but havent had the chance to try out
>          the emulator." (BL)
> (#1)
> COMMENT: The digitized voice is actually Barry Leitch himself. The original
>          Xenophobe theme, was written and 4 Amiga networked using 16 channels
>          and was originally included as a tape in the package of the game. The
>          original music features the speech of Neil Young, who is now the head
>          of Origin, which could not be used in the C64 version of the game for
>          legal reasons.
> (#11)
> COMMENT: "I used a lot of filters in the loading tune [...]" (BL)
